Class Attendance

Title 34, Section 668.21, Code of Federal Regulations, provides that if a student officially
withdraws, drops out, or is expelled before his or her first day of class of a payment period, all
funds paid to a student for that payment period under the Federal Pell, FSEOG, ICL, and
Federal Perkins Loan Programs are an overpayment. Furthermore, the Secretary of USDOE
considers that a student drops out before the first day of class of a payment period if the
institution is unable to document the student’s attendance at any class during the payment
period. Our tests disclosed three instances in which class attendance was not documented,

resulting in questioned costs of $5,133 for one institution.

Santa Fe Community College. The College did not document class attendance for three

students who received Title IV, HEA program aid totaling $5,133, consisting of subsidized
FDSLP (CFDA No. 84.268) of $385, unsubsidized FDSLP of $2,928, and Federal Pell Grant
Program (CFDA No. 84.063) of $1,820 during the 1996-97 fiscal year. After this was
brought to the College’s attention, the College began developing a system to document class
attendance for students prior to the release of SFA checks. Resolution of these questioned

costs, totaling $5,133, rests with the USDOE.

Reporting

Recipients of Federal funds are required to maintain sufficient accounting records to provide
for accountability of their use of Federal moneys. Institutions are required to file several
financial reports with the USDOE for participation in SFA programs. Title 34, Section 74.21,
Code of Federal Regulations, establishes the standards for financial management systems of
recipients of Federal funds, including requirements for the institution to maintain records that
both identify adequately the source and application of Federal funds and contain information

pertaining to Federal awards, authorizations, obligations, unobligated balances, assets, outlays,

income, and interest. Specific program regulations for SFA found in Title 34, Sections 675.19
(Federal Work-Study), 676.19 (Federal Supplemental Educational Opportunity Grant), and

and maintain on a current basis financial records that report all program transactions and
include general ledger control accounts and related subsidiary accounts that identify each

Institutions For Federally-Funded Student Financial Aid Programs. Section 5.3.1.1 of The
Blue Book provides that each financial aid program will contain all or some of the illustrated
asset (including cash), liability, capital, income, and expense accounts which will be
self-balancing and will be separated completely from one another and from the general
operating funds of the institution. Within each program, the sum of the ledger accounts with
debit balances will equal the sum of the ledger accounts with credit balances. Section 5.4.1 of
The Blue Book further provides that the institution's general ledger accounts should provide a
trial balance for the Federal SFA programs which confirm that accounts receivable, program
expenditures, and the cash balance equal the amount authorized. Our review disclosed certain
reportable conditions and questioned costs totaling $22,121 as discussed in the following

subheadings. Attachment 1 provides a summary of questioned costs for Reporting.

participated in Title IV, HEA SFA programs and received approximately $2,757,092 directly
from the Federal Government for the various programs. Additionally, the College received

approximately $830,000 through the State of Florida for other Federal contracts and grants.

Our review cf the College's accounting system disclosed that the general ledger system

"fund type" level; i.e., Current - Unrestricted, Current Restricted, etc. The College’s general
ledger system did not maintain trial balances of self-balancing groups of accounts including
cash or fund equity accounts at the individual fund or department level used to account for the
Federal SFA programs or other Federal contracts and grants received indirectly through the
State of Florida. At June 30, 1997, the College reported on its ED/PMS Form 272 (FCTR),
Status of Federal Cash, a cash deficit of $81,505.61 for the various SFA programs. The
College also reported on the Federal Distributive Aid - Cash Advance Reconciliation form, a
cash deficit of $22,674.89 for Federal contracts and grants received indirectly through the

State of Florida.

In our review of the College's accounting system, we found there were no general ledger cash
accounts disclosing cash balances which agreed with the reported cash balances for these
Federal programs. Subsequent to June 30, 1997, College personnel prepared a schedule
showing the Federal cash balances at June 30, 1997. However, in the absence of timely
prepared manual or other supplemental records, the College's accounting system did not
provide the College with records that meet, on a current basis, the minimum standards for
financial management systems for recipients of Federal funds. A similar finding was noted in
audit report No. 12983, Schedule 3, paragraphs 189 through 193. We recommend that the
College take steps to resolve these record deficiencies so that it may administer its Federally-
funded programs in compliance with applicable requirements as well as good business

practices.

Fiscal Operations Report
and Application to Participate

As a participant in the campus-based Federal SFA programs for the 1996-97 award year, and
to request funding for the 1998-99 award year, an institution is required to prepare and submit
to the USDOE a Fiscal Operations Report and Application to Participate (FISAP). Our tests of
the FISAPs prepared by the State of Florida’s 10 State Universities and 28 Community
Colleges for the 1996-97 award year disclosed that the information reported on these reports
was generally supported by institution records and in compliance with related FISAP
instructions; however, at two Colleges we noted various reporting deficiencies and at one of
the Colleges we also noted the College did not provide the Federal Work-Study Program
(FWSP) institutional matching share totaling $22,121 as discussed in the following paragraphs.
The effect of these errors, if any, on the level of future Federal funding will be determined by
the USDOE.

Miami-Dade Community College. The College was unable to provide records to support

information on eligible applicants as reported on Part II, Section G, Lines 27 through 42 of the
FISAP. A similar finding was noted in audit report No. 12983, Schedule 3, paragraph 163.
We recommend that the College maintain sufficient records to document data reported on the

FISAP.

Tallahassee Community College. The College did not apply for a Title III waiver for the

1996-97 award year; therefore, the College was required to provide a 25 percent match for the

FWSP (CFDA No. 84.033). Although the school reported on the FISAP a match totaling

Special Tests and Provisions

The specific compliance requirements for special tests and provisions are unique to each
Federal program and found in the laws, regulations, and provisions of contract or grant
agreements pertaining to the program. Our review of compliance with the various
requirements categorized as special tests and provisions disclosed circumstances which are
required to be reported as audit findings pursuant to OMB Circular A-133. These findings
included reportable conditions and instances of noncompliance resulting in questioned costs.
These findings and the amounts of questioned costs are discussed in detail under the following
subheadings. Attachment 1 provides a summary of questioned costs for Special Tests and

Provisions.

Loan Disbursements

Title 34, Section 682.604(d)(2)(i), Code of Federal Regulations, states that an institution may
not deliver FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) loan proceeds to a registered student earlier than ten

days before the first day of classes of the period of enrollment for which the loan is intended.

University of South Florida. Our tests of 50 SFA recipients disclosed that for 28 FFELP

(CFDA No. 84.032) loan disbursements, we were unable to determine the date of
disbursement. With regard to FFELP loan checks received at the beginning of the Fall 1996

and Spring 1997 semesters, University personnel indicated that due to insufficient manpower,

available staff were unable to enter the date the FFELP loan checks were disbursed. Because
the disbursement dates defaulted to the first day of classes during this peak time, the school
was unable to document the actual dates FFELP checks were disbursed to students. As a
result, we were unable to determine if the University complied with the ten-day rule. We
recommend that the University revise its procedures to document the actual date that checks

are disbursed to students.

Exit Counseling

Title 34, Sections 682.604(g)(3)(ii) and 685.304(6), Code of Federal Regulations, require a
school to conduct in-person exit counseling with each FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) and FDSLP
(CFDA No. 84.268) student borrower shortly before the borrower ceases at least half-time
study at the school. If the borrower withdraws from the institution without the institution’s
prior knowledge or fails to attend the exit counseling session as scheduled, the school shall
mail written counseling material to the borrower at the borrower’s last known address within
30 days after learning that the borrower has withdrawn from school or failed to attend the

scheduled session.

Florida Agricultural and Mechanical University. In audit report No. 12983, Schedule 2,

paragraphs 15 and 16, we noted instances of noncompliance with exit counseling regulations.
Our current review disclosed exit counseling was not performed for 11 of the 50 FDSLP
(CFDA No. 84.268) student borrowers in our SFA sample. Subsequent to our review, the
University completed the exit counseling. Exit counseling was performed 1 to 251 days late.
We recommend that student borrowers be more closely monitored for enrollment changes that
require exit counseling. In order to monitor students for enrollment changes and to better
facilitate compliance with exit counseling requirements, we recommend that the process be
transferred from the Office of Student Financial Aid to the Student Accounts Office.

University officials indicated they would discuss this option.

University of South Florida. Our review of 50 SFA recipients disclosed exit counseling was

that exit counseling was not being performed for students who ceased to be enrolled on at least
a half-time basis during the Fall 1996 and Spring 1997 semesters; however, exit counseling
was performed for students who had graduated. This error occurred due to oversight.
Subsequent to our inquiry, the school performed a mass mailing during the period of August

counseling during the Fall 1996 and Spring 1997 terms. We recommend that the University
improve its procedures for conducting exit counseling for students whose enrollment drops

below half-time.

Delayed Delivery

Title 34, Section 682.604(c)(5), Code of Federal Regulations, states that if a student is enrolled
in the first year of an undergraduate program of study and the student has not previously
received an FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) loan, the institution may not release to the student for
endorsement the first installment of his or her FFELP loan until 30 days after the first day of

the student’s classes.

University of South Florida. Our review of 50 SFA recipients disclosed that FFELP (CFDA

No. 84.032) loan disbursements were not delayed for 7 of 21 students requiring delayed
delivery. Six students were still enrolled and therefore eligible for the loans at the 30-day
point of the semester. However, 1 student had withdrawn 11 days prior to the 30-day point in
the semester and would not have been eligible for the subsidized Stafford loan of $656 had the
institution complied with delayed delivery requirements. University personnel indicated they
would reimburse the lender for this loan. We recommend that the University improve its
procedures so that FFELP loan checks are delivered within the time frames established by

regulations.

Returned Checks

Title 34, Section 682.604(d)(3), Code of Federal Regulations, states that if a student does not
register for the period of enrollment for which the FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) loan was
made, or a registered student withdraws or is expelled prior to the first day of classes of the
period of enrollment for which the loan is made, or does not begin attendance on a delayed
basis as provided in Title 34, Section 682.604(b)(2)(ii), Code of Federal Regulations, the
school shall return the proceeds to the lender no later than 30 days after the first day of that

period of enrollment.

University of South Florida. Our review of 50 SFA recipients disclosed that for 17 students
whose FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) checks were not disbursed, the University did not return

the FFELP loan checks to the lenders within the prescribed time period of 30 days. Days late
ranged from 2 to 62. We recommend that the University improve its procedures to ensure that

FFELP loan checks are returned within the time frames established by regulations.

Verifications

An institution that participates in the USDOE Quality Assurance Program is exempt from
certain requirements in verifying the information on Title IV, HEA SFA applications. In lieu
of the Federal verification requirements, the institution is required to develop its own
verification criteria. Those applications meeting the institution’s verification selection criteria
must have certain information verified as specified in Title 34, Section 668.54, Code of
Federal Regulations. As discussed in the following paragraphs, our audit disclosed instances
of noncompliance at the University of Florida resulting in no net questioned costs (total costs

of $130,767 less amounts restored totaling $130,767).

University of Florida. The University of Florida participates in the USDOE’s Quality

Assurance Program. Our audit of the records of 50 SFA recipients during the 1996-97 award

22, 1998, the University had completed the verification process for 51 of the 69 students.
There was no change in status for 16 of the students. However, the University determined that
the estimated family contribution for 35 of the students had been understated, resulting in a
total of $17,482 in Title IV, HEA program assistance being incorrectly awarded and paid. On
January 27, 1998, the University placed these students on accounts receivable and reimbursed

$17,482 to the applicable Federal accounts.

Refunds

Title 34, Section 668.22(a), Code of Federal Regulations, requires the institution to return a
portion of a refund of institutional charges (e.g., tuition and fees) owed to a student to the Title
IV, HEA programs if the student received any Title IV, HEA program aid (other than FWSP)
and officially withdraws, drops out, or is expelled from the University on or after the first day
of class. Pursuant to Title 34, Section 668.22(b)(iii), Code of Federal Regulations, the
institution must calculate a pro rata refund for any student attending the institution for the first
time whose withdrawal date is on or before the 60 percent point in time in the period of
enrollment for which the student has been charged. Title 34, Section 668.22(h)(2)(iv), Code of
Federal Regulations, requires the institution to allocate and deposit the amount of the Title IV,
HEA program portion of the refund allocated to the Title IV, HEA programs, other than FWS
and FFELP loans, to the appropriate Title IV, HEA program account(s) within 30 days after
the student’s withdrawal date. Title 34, Section 682.607(c)(1), Code of Federal Regulations,
requires a school to repay a FFELP refund to the lender within 60 days of the student’s
withdrawal. Title 34, Section 668.21(b), Code of Federal Regulations, provides that if a
student unofficially withdraws and the institution cannot document attendance in at least one

class, then all Title IV, HEA SFA paid must be returned to the Title 1V, HEA account(s).

Florida Atlantic University. Six Federal Pell Grant Program (CFDA No. 84.063) refunds

University of Central Florida. Three refunds were not returned timely to the Title IV, HEA

on December 10, 1997.

Lender Notification and/or SSCR Reporting

Title 34, Section 682, Code of Federal Regulations, provides rules for participating institutions
to follow to administer the FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032). Institutions are required to comply
with these Federal regulations to demonstrate administrative capability over the SFA programs.
For example, Title 34, Sections 682 and 685, Code of Federal Regulations, describes how
FFELP and FDSLP borrowers’ enrollment status is to be monitored after disbursement. Our
tests of the Title IV, HEA SFA programs disclosed instances in which the institutions’ records

did not always demonstrate compliance with Federal regulations as described below.

Florida Agricultural and Mechanical University. Title 34, Sections 682.610(c) and

685.309(b), Code of Federal Regulations, require that an institution notify a lender (FFELP) or
the Secretary (FDSLP) within 30 days after the institution discovers that a student who has a
loan under the FFELP (CFDA No. 84.032) and/or the FDSLP (CFDA No. 84.268) has ceased
to be enrolled on at least a half-time basis for students who received a loan based on full-time
enrollment and the institution does not expect to submit its next SSCR within 60 days. In audit
report No. 12983, Schedule 2, paragraphs 17 through 20, we noted instances of noncompliance
with SSCR and Lender Notification regulations. Our current review of 30 students who were
reported on the University’s SSCR disclosed that 5 students’ enrollment status was incorrectly
reported and 1 of the 5 students was included on an SSCR that was submitted late. In addition,
13 borrowers, some with both FFELP and FDSLP loans, with reportable enrollment status
changes were not reported timely to the lender (FFELP) or Secretary (FDSLP), as applicable.
Lender notification was performed on January 8, 1997, 83 days after the enrollment change

occurred, for the one student who had FFELP loans with three different lenders.

The FDSLP borrowers with reportable enrollment changes were not reported until the
University was able to successfully transmit data via the NSLDS. During the 1996-97 award

year, the University experienced program incompatibility with the USDOE’s NSLDS. On

should eliminate the problems noted above. We recommend that the University develop and
implement procedures that would enable them to comply with those regulations governing the

FFEL and FDSL programs as noted above.

St. Petersburg Junior College. The College received SSCRs from USA Group during the

1996-97 award year. Our review indicated that the Spring 1997 report was submitted on July
24, 1997: however, the College did not retain a copy of the report. Therefore, we were unable
to determine that the students’ enrollment status was accurately reported. In order to comply
with Federal regulations and to provide audit evidence that SSCRs are accurately completed,

we recommend that the College maintain copies of the SSCRs.

Tallahassee Community College. Our review indicated that the Fall 1996 SSCR was submitted

on October 30, 1996; however, the College did not retain a copy of the report. Therefore, we
were unable to determine that the students’ enrollment status was accurately reported. In order
to comply with Federal regulations and to provide audit evidence that SSCRs are accurately

completed, we recommend that the College maintain copies of SSCRs.

Pell Adjustments

Title 34, Section 690.83, Code of Federal Regulations, provides for postdeadline increases for
Federal Pell Grant Program (CFDA No. 84.063) awards underreported by an educational
institution totaling at least $100 if disclosed during a SFA audit. The USDOE, in Dear
Colleague Letter P-97-2, dated June 1997, and related attachments, specifies the requirements
that an educational institution must meet to receive credit to its final Federal Pell Grant

Program authorization.

RESEARCH AND DEVELOPMENT CLUSTER

Matching, Level of Effort, and/or Earmarking

Cost Sharing (National Aeronautical Space
Administration (NASA) - “Center for Nonlinear
and Nonequilibrium Aeroscience” Grant - CFDA No. 43.002)

Florida Agricultural and Mechanical University. OMB Circular A-110, Subpart C, Section

23, provides that all contributions, including cash and third-party in-kind, shall be accepted as
part of the recipient’s cost-sharing or matching when such contributions are verifiable from the
recipient’s records. The National Aeronautical Space Administration (NASA) awarded a grant

to the University titled “Center for Nonlinear and Nonequilibrium Aeroscience” (CFDA No.

43.002). This Grant was awarded on a continuing basis from January 1, 1992, through
December 31, 1996, and totaled approximately $7.7 million. One of the provisions of the

Grant was that the University would pay 8 percent of the cost of the Grant.

The University indicated that its share of the Grant was to be made up of various construction
costs for the Center, private resources, and faculty time and effort in addition to that already
charged to the project. Faculty time and effort to be contributed by the University was to total
approximately $260,000 over the life of the Grant and approximately $38,000 in the 1996
calendar year. The Principal Investigator (PI) was to provide $17,777 of the $38,000 total.
The University used personnel activity reports to document cost-sharing for faculty time and
effort. Although University personnel subsequently provided personnel activity reports
indicating that the PI had provided the required time and effort to meet the cost-sharing
provision, these reports had not been prepared at the time of our inquiry in December 1997.
Written procedures for the Division of Sponsored Research did not address cost-sharing
documentation requirements. The University should strengthen its procedures to ensure that

cost-sharing documentation requirements have been met and that such documentation is on file.

Eligibility

Single Parent Program

Indian River Community College. During the 1996-97 fiscal year, the College incurred

Federal expenditures of $51,530 in the Single Parent Grant, which is part of the Vocational
Education Program. The Single Parent Grant is funded by Section 221 of the Carl D. Perkins
Vocational and Applied Technology Education Funds and provides tuition assistance,
counseling, and other services to eligible students in preparation for vocational education and
training. Title 34, Section 400.4, Code of Federal Regulations, provides eligibility criteria for
single parents. Title 34, Section 403.81, Code of Federal Regulations, directs how funds are
to be used under the Single Parent Program. The College uses the Florida Department of
Education’s (FDOE) Computer-Assisted Management Program (CAMP) to report enrolled
students and potential students. Page X.1 of the CAMP Manual provides definitions and

examples of services.

The Single Parent Grant is funded based on performance measures, including the number of
new and continuing students enrolled in vocational programs, and the number of students who
complete the program. During the 1996-97 fiscal year, the College reported a total of 307 new
and continuing enrolled students. Our tests of 37 reported enrolled students revealed 5
students who were not registered for classes during the 1996-97 fiscal year and 14 continuing
students who received funding from sources other than the Single Parent Grant and whose files
contained no evidence of counseling or other services provided by Single Parent Grant
personnel. According to the Women’s Program Coordinator, the students were monitored by
inquiry of the registration system each semester to determine if the student is actively taking

classes toward an employment goal. However, unless the student receives direct financial

support from the grant or receives other documented services (i.e., counseling), the student
should not be counted as enrolled/served. As the level of funding for this program is

Individualized Manpower Training/System
of Applied Individualized Learning

Indian River Community College. During the 1996-97 fiscal year, the College incurred

Federal expenditures of $127,781 in the Individualized Manpower Training/System of Applied
Individualized Learning component of a block grant funded by Section 232 of the Carl D.
Perkins Vocational and Applied Technology Education Funds. The grant provides counseling,
diagnostic testing and evaluation, remediation, tutorial assistance, and post test evaluations
specifically geared to serve academically or economically disadvantaged students. Title 34,
Section 400.4, Code of Federal Regulations, provides eligibility criteria for determining
economically or academically disadvantaged individuals. An academically disadvantaged
individual is defined as a dropout from or potential dropouts from secondary schools,
individuals who score below grade level on a standardized test, or who fail to attain minimum
academic competencies. The College uses the Test of Adult Basic Education (TABE) to
determine if clients are below grade level and thus eligible for services. The results of the

TABE are documented on a Registration Information form.

Our tests of eight students disclosed that five students received tutorial assistance at the CPI lab
but did not take the TABE test. In response to our inquiries as to how these students were
assessed as eligible, the Assistant Dean of Developmental Education stated that “Delivery of
tutorial assistance includes recognition of failure to attain minimum academic competencies.”

However, College personnel did not provide documentation of the determination that these

JOB TRAINING PARTNERSHIP ACT (CFDA NO. 17.250)

Allowable Costs/Cost Principles

Allocation of Costs

Office of Management and Budget Circular (OMB) A-21, Principles for Determining Costs
Applicable to Grants, Contracts, and Other Agreements with Educational Institutions, provides
that an institution is responsible for ensuring that costs charged to a grant are allowable,
allocable, and reasonable under these cost principles. Section C.4. of this Circular provides
that a cost is allocable to a particular cost objective if the goods or services involved are
chargeable or assignable to such cost objective in accordance with relative benefits received or
other equitable relationship. Further, a cost is allocable to a grant if it is incurred solely to
advance the work under the grant, it benefits both the grant and other work of the institution in
proportions that can be approximated through use of reasonable methods, or it is necessary to
the overall operation of the institution and is deemed to be assignable in part to the grant
agreement. The Circular also describes the types of documentation acceptable for salaries,
which includes after the fact activity records reflecting the distribution of activity expended by
employees and plan confirmation under which the distribution of salaries is based on budgeted,

planned, or assigned work activity.

South Florida Community College. The College’s JTPA contracts included an Administrative

Plan which provided that indirect expenditures, including salaries, were to be allocated based
on student enrollment statistics (students served under the JTPA grants). However, College

personnel did not prepare the necessary student enrollment statistics with which to make the

allocation. Consequently, the College allocated costs using a cost pool for all JTPA
expenditures that could not be directly assigned to a specific JTPA project. Under this
method, indirect JTPA expenditures are charged to the JTPA Cost Pool and, at the end of each
month, are assigned to specific JTPA projects based on the ratio of direct expenditures for each
project to the total direct expenditures for all JTPA projects for the month. Our audit tests
disclosed payroll and nonpayroll expenditures totaling $236,170, which had been allocated to
the various JTPA projects using this methodology. Because student enrollment statistics were
not prepared by the College, we cannot determine to what extent costs allocated under this
allocation method differ from the costs that should have been allocated under the
Administrative Plan. Accordingly, costs allocated under the JTPA Cost Pool method may

represent questioned costs subject to disallowance by the USDOL.

Under its JTPA contracts and the USDOL Technical Assistance Guide, the College reported
stand-in costs on its quarterly expenditure reports filed with the grantor. Stand-in costs are
costs that can be charged to the grant under Federal cost principles, but will not be paid to the
College because they are in excess of the Federal share of the grant. The purpose of reporting
these costs is to substitute these costs in the event that costs charged to the grant are disallowed
at a later time. If costs charged to the grant are disallowed, the College would use the stand-in
costs to replace the disallowed costs. Our review disclosed that stand-in costs reported to the
USDOL consisted of administrative salary expenditures totaling $106,251 allocated to the
various JTPA grants by using a percentage of time administrative staff worked on JTPA
projects. However, College personnel were unable to provide either activity records that
showed the time spent by these staff on JTPA projects or other documentation described in
OMB Circular A-21 to support the percentages used to allocate the salaries. Accordingly,
these salary costs may not meet the requirements for stand-in costs and, as such, may not be

used to replace any costs disallowed by the USDOL. We recommend that the College prepare

Manatee Community College. Our tests disclosed salaries totaling $9,736 were charged to the

Employment and Training Assistance - Dislocated Workers Program (CFDA No. 17.246);
however, these salaries were direct costs of the JTPA Program (CFDA No. 17.250) and
should have been charged to that Program as required by the provisions of OMB Circular A-
21. This error resulted from the College not having procedures in place to review supporting
documentation of direct charges of employees working in the One Stop and Summer Youth
Program. We recommend that the College establish procedures to monitor the direct charges

of employees to ensure that direct costs are charged to the program receiving the benefit.
